 7→</p>\\n  8→\\n  9→# Letta (formerly MemGPT)\\n 10→\\n 11→Letta is the platform for building stateful agents: open AI with advanced memory that can learn and self-improve over time.\\n 12→\\n 13→### Quicklinks:\\n 14→* [**Developer Documentation**](https://docs.letta.com): Learn how create agents that learn using Python / TypeScript\\n 15→* [**Agent Development Environment ADE, available on MacOS and Windows\\n 17→* [**Letta Cloud**](https://app.letta.com/): The fastest way to try Letta, with agents running in the cloud\\n 18→\\n 19→\\n 20→## Get started\\n 21→\\n 22→### [One-Shot ✨ Vibecoding ⚡️ Prompts](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta/blob/main/fern/pages/getting-started/prompts.mdx)\\n 23→\\n 24→Or install the Letta SDK (available for both Python and TypeScript):\\n 25→\\n 26→### [Python SDK](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-python)\\n 27→```sh\\n 28→pip install letta-client\\n 29→```\\n 30→\\n 31→### [TypeScript / Node.js SDK](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-node)\\n 32→```sh\\n 33→npm install @letta-ai/letta-client\\n 34→```\\n 35→\\n 36→## Simple Hello World example\\n 37→\\n 38→In the example below, we'll create a stateful agent with two memory blocks, one for itself (the `persona` block), and one for the human. We'll initialize the `human` memory block with incorrect information, and correct agent in our first message - which will trigger the agent to update its own memory with a tool call.\\n 39→\\n 40→*To run the examples, you'll need to get a `LETTA_API_KEY` from [Letta Cloud](https://app.letta.com/api-keys), or run your own self-hosted server (see [our guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/selfhosting))*\\n 41→\\n 42→\\n 43→### Python\\n 44→```python\\n 45→from letta_client import Letta\\n 46→import os\\n 47→\\n 48→# Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 49→client = Letta(token=os.getenv(\\\"LETTA_API_KEY\\\"))\\n 50→# client = Letta(base_url=\\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding=\\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\")  # if self-hosting, set base_url and embedding\\n 51→\\n 52→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n 53→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 54→    memory_blocks=[\\n 55→        {\\n 56→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"human\\\",\\n 57→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"The human's name is Chad. They like vibe coding.\\\"\\n 58→        },\\n 59→        {\\n 60→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\",\\n 61→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"My name is Sam, a helpful assistant.\\\"\\n 62→        }\\n 63→    ],\\n 64→    tools=[\\\"web_search\\\", \\\"run_code\\\"]\\n 65→)\\n 66→\\n 67→print(agent_state.id)\\n 68→# agent-d9be...0846\\n 69→\\n 70→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n 71→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n 72→    messages=[\\n 73→        {\\n 74→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n 75→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"Hey, nice to meet you, my name is Brad.\\\"\\n 76→        }\\n 77→    ]\\n 78→)\\n 79→\\n 80→# the agent will think, then edit its memory using a tool\\n 81→for message in response.messages:\\n 82→    print(message)\\n 83→```\\n 84→\\n 85→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n 86→```typescript\\n 87→import { LettaClient } from '@letta-ai/letta-client'\\n 88→\\n 89→// Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 90→const client = new LettaClient({ token: process.env.LETTA_API_KEY });\\n 91→// const client = new LettaClient({ baseUrl: \\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding: \\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\" });  // if self-hosting\\n 92→\\n 93→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n 94→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 95→    memoryBlocks: [\\n 96→        {\\n 97→          label: \\\"human\\\",\\n 98→          value: \\\"The human's name is Chad. They like vibe coding.\\\"\\n 99→        },\\n100→        {\\n101→          label: \\\"persona\\\",\\n102→          value: \\\"My name is Sam, a helpful assistant.\\\"\\n103→        }\\n104→    ],\\n105→    tools: [\\\"web_search\\\", \\\"run_code\\\"]\\n106→});\\n107→\\n108→console.log(agentState.id);\\n109→// agent-d9be...0846\\n110→\\n111→const response = await client.agents.messages.create(\\n112→    agentState.id, {\\n113→        messages: [\\n114→            {\\n115→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n116→                content: \\\"Hey, nice to meet you, my name is Brad.\\\"\\n117→            }\\n118→        ]\\n119→    }\\n120→);\\n121→\\n122→// the agent will think, then edit its memory using a tool\\n123→for (const message of response.messages) {\\n124→    console.log(message);\\n125→}\\n126→```\\n127→\\n128→## Core concepts in Letta:\\n129→\\n130→Letta is made by the creators of [MemGPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08560), a research paper that introduced the concept of the \\\"LLM Operating System\\\" for memory management. The core concepts in Letta for designing stateful agents follow the MemGPT LLM OS principles:\\n131→\\n132→1. [**Memory Hierarchy**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory): Agents have self-editing memory that is split between in-context memory and out-of-context memory\\n133→2. [**Memory Blocks**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks): The agent's in-context memory is composed of persistent editable **memory blocks**\\n134→3. [**Agentic Context Engineering**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/context-engineering): Agents control the context window by using tools to edit, delete, or search for memory\\n135→4. [**Perpetual Self-Improving Agents**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/overview): Every \\\"agent\\\" is a single entity that has a perpetual (infinite) message history\\n136→\\n137→## Multi-agent shared memory ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/multi-agent-shared-memory))\\n138→\\n139→A single memory block can be attached to multiple agents, allowing to extremely powerful multi-agent shared memory setups.\\n140→For example, you can create two agents that have their own independent memory blocks in addition to a shared memory block.\\n141→\\n142→### Python\\n143→```python\\n144→# create a shared memory block\\n145→shared_block = client.blocks.create(\\n146→    label=\\\"organization\\\",\\n147→    description=\\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n148→    value=\\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n149→)\\n150→\\n151→# create a supervisor agent\\n152→supervisor_agent = client.agents.create(\\n153→    model=\\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n154→    # blocks created for this agent\\n155→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a supervisor\\\"}],\\n156→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n157→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n158→)\\n159→\\n160→# create a worker agent\\n161→worker_agent = client.agents.create(\\n162→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n163→    # blocks created for this agent\\n164→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a worker\\\"}],\\n165→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n166→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n167→)\\n168→```\\n169→\\n170→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n171→```typescript\\n172→// create a shared memory block\\n173→const sharedBlock = await client.blocks.create({\\n174→    label: \\\"organization\\\",\\n175→    description: \\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n176→    value: \\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n177→});\\n178→\\n179→// create a supervisor agent\\n180→const supervisorAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n181→    model: \\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n182→    // blocks created for this agent\\n183→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a supervisor\\\" }],\\n184→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n185→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n186→});\\n187→\\n188→// create a worker agent\\n189→const workerAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n190→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n191→    // blocks created for this agent\\n192→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a worker\\\" }],\\n193→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n194→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n195→});\\n196→```\\n197→\\n198→## Sleep-time agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/architectures/sleeptime))\\n199→\\n200→In Letta, you can create special **sleep-time agents** that share the memory of your primary agents, but run in the background (like an agent's \\\"subconcious\\\"). You can think of sleep-time agents as a special form of multi-agent architecture.\\n201→\\n202→To enable sleep-time agents for your agent, set the `enable_sleeptime` flag to true when creating your agent. This will automatically create a sleep-time agent in addition to your main agent which will handle the memory editing, instead of your primary agent.\\n203→\\n204→### Python\\n205→```python\\n206→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n207→    ...\\n208→    enable_sleeptime=True,  # <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n209→)\\n210→```\\n211→\\n212→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n213→```typescript\\n214→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n215→    ...\\n216→    enableSleeptime: true  // <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n217→});\\n218→```\\n219→\\n220→## Saving and sharing agents with Agent File (`.af`) ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/agent-file))\\n221→\\n222→In Letta, all agent data is persisted to disk (Postgres or SQLite), and can be easily imported and exported using the open source [Agent File](https://github.com/letta-ai/agent-file) (`.af`) file format. You can use Agent File to checkpoint your agents, as well as move your agents (and their complete state/memories) between different Letta servers, e.g. between self-hosted Letta and Letta Cloud.\\n223→\\n224→<details>\\n225→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n226→\\n227→### Python\\n228→```python\\n229→# Import your .af file from any location\\n230→agent_state = client.agents.import_agent_serialized(file=open(\\\"/path/to/agent/file.af\\\", \\\"rb\\\"))\\n231→\\n232→print(f\\\"Imported agent: {agent.id}\\\")\\n233→\\n234→# Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n235→schema = client.agents.export_agent_serialized(agent_id=\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\")\\n236→```\\n237→\\n238→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n239→```typescript\\n240→import { readFileSync } from 'fs';\\n241→import { Blob } from 'buffer';\\n242→\\n243→// Import your .af file from any location\\n244→const file = new Blob([readFileSync('/path/to/agent/file.af')])\\n245→const agentState = await client.agents.importAgentSerialized(file, {})\\n246→\\n247→console.log(`Imported agent: ${agentState.id}`);\\n248→\\n249→// Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n250→const schema = await client.agents.exportAgentSerialized(\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\");\\n251→```\\n252→</details>\\n253→\\n254→## Model Context Protocol (MCP) and custom tools ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/mcp/overview))\\n255→\\n256→Letta has rich support for MCP tools (Letta acts as an MCP client), as well as custom Python tools.\\n257→MCP servers can be easily added within the Agent Development Environment (ADE) tool manager UI, as well as via the SDK:\\n258→\\n259→\\n260→<details>\\n261→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n262→\\n263→### Python\\n264→```python\\n265→# List tools from an MCP server\\n266→tools = client.tools.list_mcp_tools_by_server(mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\")\\n267→\\n268→# Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n269→tool = client.tools.add_mcp_tool(\\n270→    mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\",\\n271→    mcp_tool_name=\\\"get_weather\\\"\\n272→)\\n273→\\n274→# Create agent with MCP tool guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/filesystem))\\n325→\\n326→Letta’s filesystem allow you to easily connect your agents to external files, for example: research papers, reports, medical records, or any other data in common text formats (`.pdf`, `.txt`, `.md`, `.json`, etc).\\n327→Once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent will be able to use filesystem tools (`open_file`, `grep_file`, `search_file`) to browse the files to search for information.\\n328→\\n329→<details>\\n330→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n331→\\n332→### Python\\n333→```python\\n334→# create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n335→folder = client.folders.create(\\n336→    name=\\\"my_folder\\\"\\n337→)\\n338→\\n339→# upload a file into the folder\\n340→job = client.folders.files.upload(\\n341→    folder_id=folder.id,\\n342→    file=open(\\\"my_file.txt\\\", \\\"rb\\\")\\n343→)\\n344→\\n345→# wait until the job is completed\\n346→while True:\\n347→    job = client.jobs.retrieve(job.id)\\n348→    if job.status == \\\"completed\\\":\\n349→        break\\n350→    elif job.status == \\\"failed\\\":\\n351→        raise ValueError(f\\\"Job failed: {job.metadata}\\\")\\n352→    print(f\\\"Job status: {job.status}\\\")\\n353→    time.sleep(1)\\n354→\\n355→# once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent can see all files in it\\n356→client.agents.folders.attach(agent_id=agent.id, folder_id=folder.id)\\n357→\\n358→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n359→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n360→    messages=[\\n361→        {\\n362→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n363→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n364→        }\\n365→    ]\\n366→)\\n367→\\n368→for message in response.messages:\\n369→    print(message)\\n370→```\\n371→\\n372→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n373→```typescript\\n374→// create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n375→const folder = await client.folders.create({\\n376→    name: \\\"my_folder\\\"\\n377→});\\n378→\\n379→// upload a file into the folder\\n380→const uploadJob = await client.folders.files.upload(\\n381→    createReadStream(\\\"my_file.txt\\\"),\\n382→    folder.id,\\n383→);\\n384→console.log(\\\"file uploaded\\\")\\n385→\\n386→// wait until the job is completed\\n387→while (true) {\\n388→    const job = guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/long-running))\\n427→\\n428→When agents need to execute multiple tool calls or perform complex operations (like deep research, data analysis, or multi-step workflows), processing time can vary significantly. Letta supports both a background mode (with resumable streaming) as well as an async mode (with polling) to enable robust long-running agent executions.\\n429→\\n430→\\n431→<details>\\n432→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n433→\\n434→### Python\\n435→```python\\n436→stream = client.agents.messages.create_stream(\\n437→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n438→    messages=[\\n439→      {\\n440→        \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n441→        \\\"content\\\": \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n442→      }\\n443→    ],\\n444→    stream_tokens=True,\\n445→    background=True,\\n446→)\\n447→run_id = None\\n448→last_seq_id = None\\n449→for chunk in stream:\\n450→    if hasattr(chunk, \\\"run_id\\\") and hasattr(chunk, \\\"seq_id\\\"):\\n451→        run_id = chunk.run_id       # Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n452→        last_seq_id = chunk.seq_id  # Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n453→    print(chunk)\\n454→\\n455→# If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id:\\n456→for chunk in client.runs.stream(run_id, starting_after=last_seq_id):\\n457→    print(chunk)\\n458→```\\n459→\\n460→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n461→```typescript\\n462→const stream = await client.agents.messages.createStream({\\n463→    agentId: agentState.id,\\n464→    requestBody: {\\n465→        messages: [\\n466→            {\\n467→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n468→                content: \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n469→            }\\n470→        ],\\n471→        streamTokens: true,\\n472→        background: true,\\n473→    }\\n474→});\\n475→\\n476→let runId = null;\\n477→let lastSeqId = null;\\n478→for await (const chunk of stream) {\\n479→    if (chunk.run_id && chunk.seq_id) {\\n480→        runId = chunk.run_id;      // Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n481→        lastSeqId = chunk.seq_id; // Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n482→    }\\n483→    console.log(chunk);\\n484→}\\n485→\\n486→// If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id\\n487→for await (const chunk of client.runs.stream(runId, {startingAfter: lastSeqId})) {\\n488→    console.log(chunk);\\n489→}\\n490→```\\n491→</details>\\n492→\\n493→## Using local models\\n494→\\n495→Letta is model agnostic and supports using local model providers such as [Ollama](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/ollama) and [LM Studio](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/lmstudio). You can also easily swap models inside an agent after the agent has been created, by modifying the agent state with the new model provider via the SDK or in the ADE.\\n496→\\n497→## Development (only needed if you need to modify the server code)\\n498→\\n499→*Note: this repostory contains the source code for the core Letta service (API server), not the client SDKs. stateful agent with two memory blocks, one for itself (the `persona` block), and one for the human. We'll initialize the `human` memory block with incorrect information, and correct agent in our first message - which will trigger the agent to update its own memory with a tool call.\\n 39→\\n 40→*To run the examples, you'll need to get a `LETTA_API_KEY` from [Letta Cloud](https://app.letta.com/api-keys), or run your own self-hosted server (see [our guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/selfhosting))*\\n 41→\\n 42→\\n 43→### Python\\n 44→```python\\n 45→from letta_client import Letta\\n 46→import os\\n 47→\\n 48→# Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 49→client = Letta(token=os.getenv(\\\"LETTA_API_KEY\\\"))\\n 50→# client = Letta(base_url=\\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding=\\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\")  # if self-hosting, set base_url and embedding\\n 51→\\n 52→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n 53→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 54→    memory_blocks=[\\n 55→        {\\n 56→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"human\\\",\\n 57→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"The human's name is Chad. They like vibe coding.\\\"\\n 58→        },\\n 59→        {\\n 60→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\",\\n 61→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"My name is Sam, a helpful assistant.\\\"\\n 62→        }\\n 63→    ],\\n 64→    tools=[\\\"web_search\\\", \\\"run_code\\\"]\\n 65→)\\n 66→\\n 67→print(agent_state.id)\\n 68→# agent-d9be...0846\\n 69→\\n 70→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n 71→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n 72→    messages=[\\n 73→        {\\n 74→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n 75→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"Hey, nice to meet you, my name is Brad.\\\"\\n 76→        }\\n 77→    ]\\n 78→)\\n 79→\\n 80→# the agent will think, then edit its memory using a tool\\n 81→for message in response.messages:\\n 82→    print(message)\\n 83→```\\n 84→\\n 85→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n 86→```typescript\\n 87→import { LettaClient } from '@letta-ai/letta-client'\\n 88→\\n 89→// Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 90→const client = new LettaClient({ token: process.env.LETTA_API_KEY });\\n 91→// const client = new LettaClient({ baseUrl: \\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding: \\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\" });  // if self-hosting\\n 92→\\n 93→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n 94→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 95→    memoryBlocks: [\\n 96→        {\\n 97→          label: \\\"human\\\",\\n 98→          value: \\\"The human's name is Chad. They like vibe coding.\\\"\\n 99→        },\\n100→        {\\n101→          label: \\\"persona\\\",\\n102→          value: \\\"My name is Sam, a helpful assistant.\\\"\\n103→        }\\n104→    ],\\n105→    tools: [\\\"web_search\\\", \\\"run_code\\\"]\\n106→});\\n107→\\n108→console.log(agentState.id);\\n109→// agent-d9be...0846\\n110→\\n111→const response = await client.agents.messages.create(\\n112→    agentState.id, {\\n113→        messages: [\\n114→            {\\n115→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n116→                content: \\\"Hey, nice to meet you, my name is Brad.\\\"\\n117→            }\\n118→        ]\\n119→    }\\n120→);\\n121→\\n122→// the agent will think, then edit its memory using a tool\\n123→for (const message of response.messages) {\\n124→    console.log(message);\\n125→}\\n126→```\\n127→\\n128→## Core concepts in Letta:\\n129→\\n130→Letta is made by the creators of [MemGPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08560), a research paper that introduced the concept of the \\\"LLM Operating System\\\" for memory management. The core concepts in Letta for designing stateful agents follow the MemGPT LLM OS principles:\\n131→\\n132→1. [**Memory Hierarchy**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory): Agents have self-editing memory that is split between in-context memory and out-of-context memory\\n133→2. [**Memory Blocks**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks): The agent's in-context memory is composed of persistent editable **memory blocks**\\n134→3. [**Agentic Context Engineering**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/context-engineering): Agents control the context window by using tools to edit, delete, or search for memory\\n135→4. [**Perpetual Self-Improving Agents**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/overview): Every \\\"agent\\\" is a single entity that has a perpetual (infinite) message history\\n136→\\n137→## Multi-agent shared memory ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/multi-agent-shared-memory))\\n138→\\n139→A single memory block can be attached to multiple agents, allowing to extremely powerful multi-agent shared memory setups.\\n140→For example, you can create two agents that have their own independent memory blocks in addition to a shared memory block.\\n141→\\n142→### Python\\n143→```python\\n144→# create a shared memory block\\n145→shared_block = client.blocks.create(\\n146→    label=\\\"organization\\\",\\n147→    description=\\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n148→    value=\\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n149→)\\n150→\\n151→# create a supervisor agent\\n152→supervisor_agent = client.agents.create(\\n153→    model=\\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n154→    # blocks created for this agent\\n155→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a supervisor\\\"}],\\n156→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n157→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n158→)\\n159→\\n160→# create a worker agent\\n161→worker_agent = client.agents.create(\\n162→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n163→    # blocks created for this agent\\n164→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a worker\\\"}],\\n165→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n166→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n167→)\\n168→```\\n169→\\n170→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n171→```typescript\\n172→// create a shared memory block\\n173→const sharedBlock = await client.blocks.create({\\n174→    label: \\\"organization\\\",\\n175→    description: \\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n176→    value: \\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n177→});\\n178→\\n179→// create a supervisor agent\\n180→const supervisorAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n181→    model: \\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n182→    // blocks created for this agent\\n183→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a supervisor\\\" }],\\n184→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n185→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n186→});\\n187→\\n188→// create a worker agent\\n189→const workerAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n190→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n191→    // blocks created for this agent\\n192→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a worker\\\" }],\\n193→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n194→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n195→});\\n196→```\\n197→\\n198→## Sleep-time agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/architectures/sleeptime))\\n199→\\n200→In Letta, you can create special **sleep-time agents** that share the memory of your primary agents, but run in the background (like an agent's \\\"subconcious\\\"). You can think of sleep-time agents as a special form of multi-agent architecture.\\n201→\\n202→To enable sleep-time agents for your agent, set the `enable_sleeptime` flag to true when creating your agent. This will automatically create a sleep-time agent in addition to your main agent which will handle the memory editing, instead of your primary agent.\\n203→\\n204→### Python\\n205→```python\\n206→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n207→    ...\\n208→    enable_sleeptime=True,  # <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n209→)\\n210→```\\n211→\\n212→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n213→```typescript\\n214→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n215→    ...\\n216→    enableSleeptime: true  // <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n217→});\\n218→```\\n219→\\n220→## Saving and sharing agents with Agent File (`.af`) ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/agent-file))\\n221→\\n222→In Letta, all agent data is persisted to disk (Postgres or SQLite), and can be easily imported and exported using the open source [Agent File](https://github.com/letta-ai/agent-file) (`.af`) file format. You can use Agent File to checkpoint your agents, as well as move your agents (and their complete state/memories) between different Letta servers, e.g. between self-hosted Letta and Letta Cloud.\\n223→\\n224→<details>\\n225→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n226→\\n227→### Python\\n228→```python\\n229→# Import your .af file from any location\\n230→agent_state = client.agents.import_agent_serialized(file=open(\\\"/path/to/agent/file.af\\\", \\\"rb\\\"))\\n231→\\n232→print(f\\\"Imported agent: {agent.id}\\\")\\n233→\\n234→# Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n235→schema = client.agents.export_agent_serialized(agent_id=\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\")\\n236→```\\n237→\\n238→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n239→```typescript\\n240→import { readFileSync } from 'fs';\\n241→import { Blob } from 'buffer';\\n242→\\n243→// Import your .af file from any location\\n244→const file = new Blob([readFileSync('/path/to/agent/file.af')])\\n245→const agentState = await client.agents.importAgentSerialized(file, {})\\n246→\\n247→console.log(`Imported agent: ${agentState.id}`);\\n248→\\n249→// Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n250→const schema = await client.agents.exportAgentSerialized(\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\");\\n251→```\\n252→</details>\\n253→\\n254→## Model Context Protocol (MCP) and custom tools ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/mcp/overview))\\n255→\\n256→Letta has rich support for MCP tools (Letta acts as an MCP client), as well as custom Python tools.\\n257→MCP servers can be easily added within the Agent Development Environment (ADE) tool manager UI, as well as via the SDK:\\n258→\\n259→\\n260→<details>\\n261→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n262→\\n263→### Python\\n264→```python\\n265→# List tools from an MCP server\\n266→tools = client.tools.list_mcp_tools_by_server(mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\")\\n267→\\n268→# Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n269→tool = client.tools.add_mcp_tool(\\n270→    mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\",\\n271→    mcp_tool_name=\\\"get_weather\\\"\\n272→)\\n273→\\n274→# Create agent with MCP tool attached\\n275→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n276→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4o-mini\\\",\\n277→    tool_ids=[tool.id]\\n278→)\\n279→\\n280→# Or attach tools to an existing agent\\n281→client.agents.tool.attach(\\n282→    agent_id=agent_state.id\\n283→    tool_id=tool.id\\n284→)\\n285→\\n286→# Use the agent with MCP tools\\n287→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n288→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n289→    messages=[\\n290→        {\\n291→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n292→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"Use the weather tool to check the forecast\\\"\\n293→        }\\n294→    ]\\n295→)\\n296→```\\n297→\\n298→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n299→```typescript\\n300→// List tools from an MCP server\\n301→const tools = await client.tools.listMcpToolsByServer(\\\"weather-server\\\");\\n302→\\n303→// Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n304→const tool = await client.tools.addMcpTool(\\\"weather-server\\\", \\\"get_weather\\\");\\n305→\\n306→// Create agent with MCP tool\\n307→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n308→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4o-mini\\\",\\n309→    toolIds: [tool.id]\\n310→});\\n311→\\n312→// Use the agent with MCP tools\\n313→const response = await client.agents.messages.create(agentState.id, {\\n314→    messages: [\\n315→        {\\n316→            role: \\\"user\\\",\\n317→            content: \\\"Use the weather tool to check the forecast\\\"\\n318→        }\\n319→    ]\\n320→});\\n321→```\\n322→</details>\\n323→\\n324→## Filesystem ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/filesystem))\\n325→\\n326→Letta’s filesystem allow you to easily connect your agents to external files, for example: research papers, reports, medical records, or any other data in common text formats (`.pdf`, `.txt`, `.md`, `.json`, etc).\\n327→Once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent will be able to use filesystem tools (`open_file`, `grep_file`, `search_file`) to browse the files to search for information.\\n328→\\n329→<details>\\n330→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n331→\\n332→### Python\\n333→```python\\n334→# create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n335→folder = client.folders.create(\\n336→    name=\\\"my_folder\\\"\\n337→)\\n338→\\n339→# upload a file into the folder\\n340→job = client.folders.files.upload(\\n341→    folder_id=folder.id,\\n342→    file=open(\\\"my_file.txt\\\", \\\"rb\\\")\\n343→)\\n344→\\n345→# wait until the job is completed\\n346→while True:\\n347→    job = client.jobs.retrieve(job.id)\\n348→    if job.status == \\\"completed\\\":\\n349→        break\\n350→    elif job.status == \\\"failed\\\":\\n351→        raise ValueError(f\\\"Job failed: {job.metadata}\\\")\\n352→    print(f\\\"Job status: {job.status}\\\")\\n353→    time.sleep(1)\\n354→\\n355→# once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent can see all files in it\\n356→client.agents.folders.attach(agent_id=agent.id, folder_id=folder.id)\\n357→\\n358→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n359→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n360→    messages=[\\n361→        {\\n362→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n363→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n364→        }\\n365→    ]\\n366→)\\n367→\\n368→for message in response.messages:\\n369→    print(message)\\n370→```\\n371→\\n372→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n373→```typescript\\n374→// create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n375→const folder = await client.folders.create({\\n376→    name: \\\"my_folder\\\"\\n377→});\\n378→\\n379→// upload a file into the folder\\n380→const uploadJob = await client.folders.files.upload(\\n381→    createReadStream(\\\"my_file.txt\\\"),\\n382→    folder.id,\\n383→);\\n384→console.log(\\\"file uploaded\\\")\\n385→\\n386→// wait until the job is completed\\n387→while (true) {\\n388→    const job = Error(`Job failed: ${job.metadata}`);\\n393→    }\\n394→    console.log(`Job status: ${job.status}`);\\n395→    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 1000));\\n396→}\\n397→\\n398→// list files in the folder\\n399→const files = await client.folders.files.list(folder.id);\\n400→console.log(`Files in folder: ${files}`);\\n401→\\n402→// list passages in the folder\\n403→const passages = await client.folders.passages.list(folder.id);\\n404→console.log(`Passages in folder: ${passages}`);\\n405→\\n406→// once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent can see all files in it\\n407→await             role: \\\"user\\\",\\n414→                content: \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n415→            }\\n416→        ]\\n417→    }\\n418→);\\n419→\\n420→for (const message of response.messages) {\\n421→    console.log(message);\\n422→}\\n423→```\\n424→</details>\\n425→\\n426→## Long-running agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/long-running))\\n427→\\n428→When agents need to execute multiple tool calls or perform complex operations (like deep research, data analysis, or multi-step workflows), processing time can vary significantly. Letta supports both a background mode (with resumable streaming) as well as an async mode (with polling) to enable robust long-running agent executions.\\n429→\\n430→\\n431→<details>\\n432→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n433→\\n434→### Python\\n435→```python\\n436→stream = client.agents.messages.create_stream(\\n437→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n438→    messages=[\\n439→      {\\n440→        \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n441→        \\\"content\\\": \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n442→      }\\n443→    ],\\n444→    stream_tokens=True,\\n445→    background=True,\\n446→)\\n447→run_id = None\\n448→last_seq_id = None\\n449→for chunk in stream:\\n450→    if hasattr(chunk, \\\"run_id\\\") and hasattr(chunk, \\\"seq_id\\\"):\\n451→        run_id = chunk.run_id       # Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n452→        last_seq_id = chunk.seq_id  # Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n453→    print(chunk)\\n454→\\n455→# If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id:\\n456→for chunk in client.runs.stream(run_id, starting_after=last_seq_id):\\n457→    print(chunk)\\n458→```\\n459→\\n460→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n461→```typescript\\n462→const stream = await client.agents.messages.createStream({\\n463→    agentId: agentState.id,\\n464→    requestBody: {\\n465→        messages: [\\n466→            {\\n467→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n468→                content: \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n469→            }\\n470→        ],\\n471→        streamTokens: true,\\n472→        background: true,\\n473→    }\\n474→});\\n475→\\n476→let runId = null;\\n477→let lastSeqId = null;\\n478→for await (const chunk of stream) {\\n479→    if (chunk.run_id && chunk.seq_id) {\\n480→        runId = chunk.run_id;      // Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n481→        lastSeqId = chunk.seq_id; // Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n482→    }\\n483→    console.log(chunk);\\n484→}\\n485→\\n486→// If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id\\n487→for await (const chunk of client.runs.stream(runId, {startingAfter: lastSeqId})) {\\n488→    console.log(chunk);\\n489→}\\n490→```\\n491→</details>\\n492→\\n493→## Using local models\\n494→\\n495→Letta is model agnostic and supports using local model providers such as [Ollama](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/ollama) and [LM Studio](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/lmstudio). You can also easily swap models inside an agent after the agent has been created, by modifying the agent state with the new model provider via the SDK or in the ADE.\\n496→\\n497→## Development (only needed if you need to modify the server code)\\n498→\\n499→*Note: this repostory contains the source code for the core Letta service (API server), not the client SDKs. The client SDKs can be found here: [Python](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-python), [TypeScript](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-node).*\\n500→\\n501→To install the Letta server from source, fork the repo, clone your fork, then use [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/getting-started/installation/) to install from inside the main directory:\\n502→```sh\\n503→cd letta\\n504→uv sync --all-extras\\n505→```\\n506→\\n507→To run the Letta server from source, use `uv run`:\\n508→```sh\\n509→uv run letta server\\n510→```\\n511→\\n512→## Contributing\\n513→\\n514→Letta is an open source project built by over a hundred contributors. There seems to be a potential issue with the `settings.letta_pg_uri` in server/db.py. It returns a default Postgres URI even when the intended database engine is SQLite.  7→</p>\\n  8→\\n  9→# Letta (formerly MemGPT)\\n 10→\\n 11→Letta is the platform for building stateful agents: open AI with advanced memory that can learn and self-improve over time.\\n 12→\\n 13→### Quicklinks:\\n 14→* [**Developer Documentation**](https://docs.letta.com): Learn how create agents that learn using Python / TypeScript\\n 15→* [**Agent Development Environment SDK](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-node)\\n 32→```sh\\n 33→npm install @letta-ai/letta-client\\n 34→```\\n 35→\\n 36→## Simple Hello World example\\n 37→\\n 38→In the example below, we'll create a stateful agent with two memory blocks, one for itself (the `persona` block), and one for the human. We'll initialize the `human` memory block with incorrect information, and correct agent in our first message - which will trigger the agent to update its own memory with a tool call.\\n 39→\\n 40→*To run the examples, you'll need to get a `LETTA_API_KEY` from [Letta Cloud](https://app.letta.com/api-keys), or run your own self-hosted server (see [our guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/selfhosting))*\\n 41→\\n 42→\\n 43→### Python\\n 44→```python\\n 45→from letta_client import Letta\\n 46→import os\\n 47→\\n 48→# Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 49→client = Letta(token=os.getenv(\\\"LETTA_API_KEY\\\"))\\n 50→# client = Letta(base_url=\\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding=\\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\")  # if self-hosting, set base_url and embedding\\n 51→\\n 52→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n 53→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 54→    memory_blocks=[\\n 55→        {\\n 56→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"human\\\",\\n 57→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"The human's name is Chad. They like vibe coding.\\\"\\n 58→        },\\n 59→        {\\n 60→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\",\\n 61→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"My name is Sam, a helpful assistant.\\\"\\n 62→        }\\n 63→    ],\\n 64→    tools=[\\\"web_search\\\", \\\"run_code\\\"]\\n 65→)\\n 66→\\n 67→print(agent_state.id)\\n 68→# agent-d9be...0846\\n 69→\\n 70→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n 71→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n 72→    messages=[\\n 73→        {\\n 74→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n 75→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"Hey, nice to meet you, my name is Brad.\\\"\\n 76→        }\\n 77→    ]\\n 78→)\\n 79→\\n 80→# the agent will think, then edit its memory using a tool\\n 81→for message in response.messages:\\n 82→    print(message)\\n 83→```\\n 84→\\n 85→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n 86→```typescript\\n 87→import { LettaClient } from '@letta-ai/letta-client'\\n 88→\\n 89→// Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 90→const client = new LettaClient({ token: process.env.LETTA_API_KEY });\\n 91→// const client = new LettaClient({ baseUrl: \\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding: \\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\" });  // if self-hosting\\n 92→\\n 93→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n 94→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 95→    memoryBlocks: [\\n 96→        {\\n 97→          label: \\\"human\\\",\\n 98→          value: \\\"The human's name is Chad. They like vibe coding.\\\"\\n 99→        },\\n100→        {\\n101→          label: \\\"persona\\\",\\n102→          value: \\\"My name is Sam, a helpful assistant.\\\"\\n103→        }\\n104→    ],\\n105→    tools: [\\\"web_search\\\", \\\"run_code\\\"]\\n106→});\\n107→\\n108→console.log(agentState.id);\\n109→// agent-d9be...0846\\n110→\\n111→const response = await client.agents.messages.create(\\n112→    agentState.id, {\\n113→        messages: [\\n114→            {\\n115→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n116→                content: \\\"Hey, nice to meet you, my name is Brad.\\\"\\n117→            }\\n118→        ]\\n119→    }\\n120→);\\n121→\\n122→// the agent will think, then edit its memory using a tool\\n123→for (const message of response.messages) {\\n124→    console.log(message);\\n125→}\\n126→```\\n127→\\n128→## Core concepts in Letta:\\n129→\\n130→Letta is made by the creators of [MemGPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08560), a research paper that introduced the concept of the \\\"LLM Operating System\\\" for memory management. The core concepts in Letta for designing stateful agents follow the MemGPT LLM OS principles:\\n131→\\n132→1. [**Memory Hierarchy**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory): Agents have self-editing memory that is split between in-context memory and out-of-context memory\\n133→2. [**Memory Blocks**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks): The agent's in-context memory is composed of persistent editable **memory blocks**\\n134→3. [**Agentic Context Engineering**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/context-engineering): Agents control the context window by using tools to edit, delete, or search for memory\\n135→4. [**Perpetual Self-Improving Agents**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/overview): Every \\\"agent\\\" is a single entity that has a perpetual (infinite) message history\\n136→\\n137→## Multi-agent shared memory ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/multi-agent-shared-memory))\\n138→\\n139→A single memory block can be attached to multiple agents, allowing to extremely powerful multi-agent shared memory setups.\\n140→For example, you can create two agents that have their own independent memory blocks in addition to a shared memory block.\\n141→\\n142→### Python\\n143→```python\\n144→# create a shared memory block\\n145→shared_block = client.blocks.create(\\n146→    label=\\\"organization\\\",\\n147→    description=\\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n148→    value=\\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n149→)\\n150→\\n151→# create a supervisor agent\\n152→supervisor_agent = client.agents.create(\\n153→    model=\\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n154→    # blocks created for this agent\\n155→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a supervisor\\\"}],\\n156→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n157→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n158→)\\n159→\\n160→# create a worker agent\\n161→worker_agent = client.agents.create(\\n162→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n163→    # blocks created for this agent\\n164→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a worker\\\"}],\\n165→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n166→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n167→)\\n168→```\\n169→\\n170→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n171→```typescript\\n172→// create a shared memory block\\n173→const sharedBlock = await client.blocks.create({\\n174→    label: \\\"organization\\\",\\n175→    description: \\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n176→    value: \\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n177→});\\n178→\\n179→// create a supervisor agent\\n180→const supervisorAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n181→    model: \\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n182→    // blocks created for this agent\\n183→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a supervisor\\\" }],\\n184→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n185→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n186→});\\n187→\\n188→// create a worker agent\\n189→const workerAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n190→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n191→    // blocks created for this agent\\n192→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a worker\\\" }],\\n193→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n194→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n195→});\\n196→```\\n197→\\n198→## Sleep-time agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/architectures/sleeptime))\\n199→\\n200→In Letta, you can create special **sleep-time agents** that share the memory of your primary agents, but run in the background (like an agent's \\\"subconcious\\\"). You can think of sleep-time agents as a special form of multi-agent architecture.\\n201→\\n202→To enable sleep-time agents for your agent, set the `enable_sleeptime` flag to true when creating your agent. This will automatically create a sleep-time agent in addition to your main agent which will handle the memory editing, instead of your primary agent.\\n203→\\n204→### Python\\n205→```python\\n206→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n207→    ...\\n208→    enable_sleeptime=True,  # <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n209→)\\n210→```\\n211→\\n212→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n213→```typescript\\n214→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n215→    ...\\n216→    enableSleeptime: true  // <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n217→});\\n218→```\\n219→\\n220→## Saving and sharing agents with Agent File (`.af`) ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/agent-file))\\n221→\\n222→In Letta, all agent data is persisted to disk (Postgres or SQLite), and can be easily imported and exported using the open source [Agent File](https://github.com/letta-ai/agent-file) (`.af`) file format. You can use Agent File to checkpoint your agents, as well as move your agents (and their complete state/memories) between different Letta servers, e.g. between self-hosted Letta and Letta Cloud.\\n223→\\n224→<details>\\n225→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n226→\\n227→### Python\\n228→```python\\n229→# Import your .af file from any location\\n230→agent_state = client.agents.import_agent_serialized(file=open(\\\"/path/to/agent/file.af\\\", \\\"rb\\\"))\\n231→\\n232→print(f\\\"Imported agent: {agent.id}\\\")\\n233→\\n234→# Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n235→schema = client.agents.export_agent_serialized(agent_id=\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\")\\n236→```\\n237→\\n238→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n239→```typescript\\n240→import { readFileSync } from 'fs';\\n241→import { Blob } from 'buffer';\\n242→\\n243→// Import your .af file from any location\\n244→const file = new Blob([readFileSync('/path/to/agent/file.af')])\\n245→const agentState = await client.agents.importAgentSerialized(file, {})\\n246→\\n247→console.log(`Imported agent: ${agentState.id}`);\\n248→\\n249→// Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n250→const schema = await client.agents.exportAgentSerialized(\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\");\\n251→```\\n252→</details>\\n253→\\n254→## Model Context Protocol (MCP) and custom tools ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/mcp/overview))\\n255→\\n256→Letta has rich support for MCP tools (Letta acts as an MCP client), as well as custom Python tools.\\n257→MCP servers can be easily added within the Agent Development Environment (ADE) tool manager UI, as well as via the SDK:\\n258→\\n259→\\n260→<details>\\n261→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n262→\\n263→### Python\\n264→```python\\n265→# List tools from an MCP server\\n266→tools = client.tools.list_mcp_tools_by_server(mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\")\\n267→\\n268→# Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n269→tool = client.tools.add_mcp_tool(\\n270→    mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\",\\n271→    mcp_tool_name=\\\"get_weather\\\"\\n272→)\\n273→\\n274→# Create agent with MCP tool attached\\n275→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n276→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4o-mini\\\",\\n277→    tool_ids=[tool.id]\\n278→)\\n279→\\n280→# Or attach tools to an existing agent\\n281→client.agents.tool.attach(\\n282→    agent_id=agent_state.id\\n283→    tool_id=tool.id\\n284→)\\n285→\\n286→# Use the agent with MCP tools\\n287→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n288→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n289→    messages=[\\n290→        {\\n291→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n292→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"Use the weather tool to check the forecast\\\"\\n293→        }\\n294→    ]\\n295→)\\n296→```\\n297→\\n298→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n299→```typescript\\n300→// List tools from an MCP server\\n301→const tools = await client.tools.listMcpToolsByServer(\\\"weather-server\\\");\\n302→\\n303→// Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n304→const tool = await client.tools.addMcpTool(\\\"weather-server\\\", \\\"get_weather\\\");\\n305→\\n306→// Create agent with MCP tool\\n307→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n308→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4o-mini\\\",\\n309→    toolIds: [tool.id]\\n310→});\\n311→\\n312→// Use the agent with MCP tools\\n313→const response = await client.agents.messages.create(agentState.id, {\\n314→    messages: [\\n315→        {\\n316→            role: \\\"user\\\",\\n317→            content: \\\"Use the weather tool to check the forecast\\\"\\n318→        }\\n319→    ]\\n320→});\\n321→```\\n322→</details>\\n323→\\n324→## Filesystem ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/filesystem))\\n325→\\n326→Letta’s filesystem allow you to easily connect your agents to external files, for example: research papers, reports, medical records, or any other data in common text formats (`.pdf`, `.txt`, `.md`, `.json`, etc).\\n327→Once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent will be able to use filesystem tools (`open_file`, `grep_file`, `search_file`) to browse the files to search for information.\\n328→\\n329→<details>\\n330→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n331→\\n332→### Python\\n333→```python\\n334→# create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n335→folder = client.folders.create(\\n336→    name=\\\"my_folder\\\"\\n337→)\\n338→\\n339→# upload a file into the folder\\n340→job = client.folders.files.upload(\\n341→    folder_id=folder.id,\\n342→    file=open(\\\"my_file.txt\\\", \\\"rb\\\")\\n343→)\\n344→\\n345→# wait until the job is completed\\n346→while True:\\n347→    job = client.jobs.retrieve(job.id)\\n348→    if job.status == \\\"completed\\\":\\n349→        break\\n350→    elif job.status == \\\"failed\\\":\\n351→        raise ValueError(f\\\"Job failed: {job.metadata}\\\")\\n352→    print(f\\\"Job status: {job.status}\\\")\\n353→    time.sleep(1)\\n354→\\n355→# once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent can see all files in it\\n356→client.agents.folders.attach(agent_id=agent.id, folder_id=folder.id)\\n357→\\n358→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n359→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n360→    messages=[\\n361→        {\\n362→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n363→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n364→        }\\n365→    ]\\n366→)\\n367→\\n368→for message in response.messages:\\n369→    print(message)\\n370→```\\n371→\\n372→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n373→```typescript\\n374→// create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n375→const folder = await client.folders.create({\\n376→    name: \\\"my_folder\\\"\\n377→});\\n378→\\n379→// upload a file into the folder\\n380→const uploadJob = await client.folders.files.upload(\\n381→    createReadStream(\\\"my_file.txt\\\"),\\n382→    folder.id,\\n383→);\\n384→console.log(\\\"file uploaded\\\")\\n385→\\n386→// wait until the job is completed\\n387→while (true) {\\n388→    const job =             role: \\\"user\\\",\\n414→                content: \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n415→            }\\n416→        ]\\n417→    }\\n418→);\\n419→\\n420→for (const message of response.messages) {\\n421→    console.log(message);\\n422→}\\n423→```\\n424→</details>\\n425→\\n426→## Long-running agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/long-running))\\n427→\\n428→When agents need to execute multiple tool calls or perform complex operations (like deep research, data analysis, or multi-step workflows), processing time can vary significantly. Letta supports both a background mode (with resumable streaming) as well as an async mode (with polling) to enable robust long-running agent executions.\\n429→\\n430→\\n431→<details>\\n432→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n433→\\n434→### Python\\n435→```python\\n436→stream = client.agents.messages.create_stream(\\n437→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n438→    messages=[\\n439→      {\\n440→        \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n441→        \\\"content\\\": \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n442→      }\\n443→    ],\\n444→    stream_tokens=True,\\n445→    background=True,\\n446→)\\n447→run_id = None\\n448→last_seq_id = None\\n449→for chunk in stream:\\n450→    if hasattr(chunk, \\\"run_id\\\") and hasattr(chunk, \\\"seq_id\\\"):\\n451→        run_id = chunk.run_id       # Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n452→        last_seq_id = chunk.seq_id  # Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n453→    print(chunk)\\n454→\\n455→# If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id:\\n456→for chunk in client.runs.stream(run_id, starting_after=last_seq_id):\\n457→    print(chunk)\\n458→```\\n459→\\n460→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n461→```typescript\\n462→const stream = await client.agents.messages.createStream({\\n463→    agentId: agentState.id,\\n464→    requestBody: {\\n465→        messages: [\\n466→            {\\n467→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n468→                content: \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n469→            }\\n470→        ],\\n471→        streamTokens: true,\\n472→        background: true,\\n473→    }\\n474→});\\n475→\\n476→let runId = null;\\n477→let lastSeqId = null;\\n478→for await (const chunk of stream) {\\n479→    if (chunk.run_id && chunk.seq_id) {\\n480→        runId = chunk.run_id;      // Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n481→        lastSeqId = chunk.seq_id; // Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n482→    }\\n483→    console.log(chunk);\\n484→}\\n485→\\n486→// If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id\\n487→for await (const chunk of client.runs.stream(runId, {startingAfter: lastSeqId})) {\\n488→    console.log(chunk);\\n489→}\\n490→```\\n491→</details>\\n492→\\n493→## Using local models\\n494→\\n495→Letta is model agnostic and supports using local model providers such as [Ollama](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/ollama) and [LM Studio](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/lmstudio). You can also easily swap models inside an agent after the agent has been created, by modifying the agent state with the new model provider via the SDK or in the ADE.\\n496→\\n497→## Development (only needed if you need to modify the server code)\\n498→\\n499→*Note: this repostory contains the source code for the core Letta service (API server), not the client SDKs. The client SDKs can be found here: [Python](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-python), [TypeScript](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-node).*\\n500→\\n501→To install the Letta server from source, fork the repo, clone your fork, then use [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/getting-started/installation/) to install from inside the main directory:\\n502→```sh\\n503→cd letta\\n504→uv sync --all-extras\\n505→```\\n506→\\n507→To run the Letta server from source, use `uv run`:\\n508→```sh\\n509→uv run letta server\\n510→```\\n511→\\n512→## Contributing\\n513→\\n514→Letta is an open source project built by over a hundred contributors. We'll initialize the `human` memory block with incorrect information, and correct agent in our first message - which will trigger the agent to update its own memory with a tool call.\\n 39→\\n 40→*To run the examples, you'll need to get a `LETTA_API_KEY` from [Letta Cloud](https://app.letta.com/api-keys), or run your own self-hosted server (see [our guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/selfhosting))*\\n 41→\\n 42→\\n 43→### Python\\n 44→```python\\n 45→from letta_client import Letta\\n 46→import os\\n 47→\\n 48→# Connect to Letta Cloud (get your API key at https://app.letta.com/api-keys)\\n 49→client = Letta(token=os.getenv(\\\"LETTA_API_KEY\\\"))\\n 50→# client = Letta(base_url=\\\"http://localhost:8283\\\", embedding=\\\"openai/text-embedding-3-small\\\")  # if self-hosting, set base_url and embedding\\n 51→\\n 52→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n 53→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1\\\",\\n 54→    memory_blocks=[\\n 55→        {\\n 56→          \\\"label\\\": \\\"human\\\",\\n 57→          \\\"value\\\": \\\"The human's name is Chad. The core concepts in Letta for designing stateful agents follow the MemGPT LLM OS principles:\\n131→\\n132→1. [**Memory Hierarchy**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory): Agents have self-editing memory that is split between in-context memory and out-of-context memory\\n133→2. [**Memory Blocks**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks): The agent's in-context memory is composed of persistent editable **memory blocks**\\n134→3. [**Agentic Context Engineering**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/context-engineering): Agents control the context window by using tools to edit, delete, or search for memory\\n135→4. [**Perpetual Self-Improving Agents**](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/overview): Every \\\"agent\\\" is a single entity that has a perpetual (infinite) message history\\n136→\\n137→## Multi-agent shared memory ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/multi-agent-shared-memory))\\n138→\\n139→A single memory block can be attached to multiple agents, allowing to extremely powerful multi-agent shared memory setups.\\n140→For example, you can create two agents that have their own independent memory blocks in addition to a shared memory block.\\n141→\\n142→### Python\\n143→```python\\n144→# create a shared memory block\\n145→shared_block = client.blocks.create(\\n146→    label=\\\"organization\\\",\\n147→    description=\\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n148→    value=\\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n149→)\\n150→\\n151→# create a supervisor agent\\n152→supervisor_agent = client.agents.create(\\n153→    model=\\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n154→    # blocks created for this agent\\n155→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a supervisor\\\"}],\\n156→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n157→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n158→)\\n159→\\n160→# create a worker agent\\n161→worker_agent = client.agents.create(\\n162→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n163→    # blocks created for this agent\\n164→    memory_blocks=[{\\\"label\\\": \\\"persona\\\", \\\"value\\\": \\\"I am a worker\\\"}],\\n165→    # pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n166→    block_ids=[shared_block.id],\\n167→)\\n168→```\\n169→\\n170→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n171→```typescript\\n172→// create a shared memory block\\n173→const sharedBlock = await client.blocks.create({\\n174→    label: \\\"organization\\\",\\n175→    description: \\\"Shared information between all agents within the organization.\\\",\\n176→    value: \\\"Nothing here yet, we should update this over time.\\\"\\n177→});\\n178→\\n179→// create a supervisor agent\\n180→const supervisorAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n181→    model: \\\"anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\\",\\n182→    // blocks created for this agent\\n183→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a supervisor\\\" }],\\n184→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n185→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n186→});\\n187→\\n188→// create a worker agent\\n189→const workerAgent = await client.agents.create({\\n190→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\\",\\n191→    // blocks created for this agent\\n192→    memoryBlocks: [{ label: \\\"persona\\\", value: \\\"I am a worker\\\" }],\\n193→    // pre-existing shared block that is \\\"attached\\\" to this agent\\n194→    blockIds: [sharedBlock.id]\\n195→});\\n196→```\\n197→\\n198→## Sleep-time agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/architectures/sleeptime))\\n199→\\n200→In Letta, you can create special **sleep-time agents** that share the memory of your primary agents, but run in the background (like an agent's \\\"subconcious\\\"). You can think of sleep-time agents as a special form of multi-agent architecture.\\n201→\\n202→To enable sleep-time agents for your agent, set the `enable_sleeptime` flag to true when creating your agent. This will automatically create a sleep-time agent in addition to your main agent which will handle the memory editing, instead of your primary agent.\\n203→\\n204→### Python\\n205→```python\\n206→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n207→    ...\\n208→    enable_sleeptime=True,  # <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n209→)\\n210→```\\n211→\\n212→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n213→```typescript\\n214→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n215→    ...\\n216→    enableSleeptime: true  // <- enable this flag to create a sleep-time agent\\n217→});\\n218→```\\n219→\\n220→## Saving and sharing agents with Agent File (`.af`) ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/agent-file))\\n221→\\n222→In Letta, all agent data is persisted to disk (Postgres or SQLite), and can be easily imported and exported using the open source [Agent File](https://github.com/letta-ai/agent-file) (`.af`) file format. You can use Agent File to checkpoint your agents, as well as move your agents (and their complete state/memories) between different Letta servers, e.g. between self-hosted Letta and Letta Cloud.\\n223→\\n224→<details>\\n225→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n226→\\n227→### Python\\n228→```python\\n229→# Import your .af file from any location\\n230→agent_state = client.agents.import_agent_serialized(file=open(\\\"/path/to/agent/file.af\\\", \\\"rb\\\"))\\n231→\\n232→print(f\\\"Imported agent: {agent.id}\\\")\\n233→\\n234→# Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n235→schema = client.agents.export_agent_serialized(agent_id=\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\")\\n236→```\\n237→\\n238→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n239→```typescript\\n240→import { readFileSync } from 'fs';\\n241→import { Blob } from 'buffer';\\n242→\\n243→// Import your .af file from any location\\n244→const file = new Blob([readFileSync('/path/to/agent/file.af')])\\n245→const agentState = await client.agents.importAgentSerialized(file, {})\\n246→\\n247→console.log(`Imported agent: ${agentState.id}`);\\n248→\\n249→// Export your agent into a serialized schema object (which you can write to a file)\\n250→const schema = await client.agents.exportAgentSerialized(\\\"<AGENT_ID>\\\");\\n251→```\\n252→</details>\\n253→\\n254→## Model Context Protocol (MCP) and custom tools ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/mcp/overview))\\n255→\\n256→Letta has rich support for MCP tools (Letta acts as an MCP client), as well as custom Python tools.\\n257→MCP servers can be easily added within the Agent Development Environment (ADE) tool manager UI, as well as via the SDK:\\n258→\\n259→\\n260→<details>\\n261→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n262→\\n263→### Python\\n264→```python\\n265→# List tools from an MCP server\\n266→tools = client.tools.list_mcp_tools_by_server(mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\")\\n267→\\n268→# Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n269→tool = client.tools.add_mcp_tool(\\n270→    mcp_server_name=\\\"weather-server\\\",\\n271→    mcp_tool_name=\\\"get_weather\\\"\\n272→)\\n273→\\n274→# Create agent with MCP tool attached\\n275→agent_state = client.agents.create(\\n276→    model=\\\"openai/gpt-4o-mini\\\",\\n277→    tool_ids=[tool.id]\\n278→)\\n279→\\n280→# Or attach tools to an existing agent\\n281→client.agents.tool.attach(\\n282→    agent_id=agent_state.id\\n283→    tool_id=tool.id\\n284→)\\n285→\\n286→# Use the agent with MCP tools\\n287→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n288→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n289→    messages=[\\n290→        {\\n291→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n292→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"Use the weather tool to check the forecast\\\"\\n293→        }\\n294→    ]\\n295→)\\n296→```\\n297→\\n298→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n299→```typescript\\n300→// List tools from an MCP server\\n301→const tools = await client.tools.listMcpToolsByServer(\\\"weather-server\\\");\\n302→\\n303→// Add a specific tool from the MCP server\\n304→const tool = await client.tools.addMcpTool(\\\"weather-server\\\", \\\"get_weather\\\");\\n305→\\n306→// Create agent with MCP tool\\n307→const agentState = await client.agents.create({\\n308→    model: \\\"openai/gpt-4o-mini\\\",\\n309→    toolIds: [tool.id]\\n310→});\\n311→\\n312→// Use the agent with MCP tools\\n313→const response = await client.agents.messages.create(agentState.id, {\\n314→    messages: [\\n315→        {\\n316→            role: \\\"user\\\",\\n317→            content: \\\"Use the weather tool to check the forecast\\\"\\n318→        }\\n319→    ]\\n320→});\\n321→```\\n322→</details>\\n323→\\n324→## Filesystem ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/filesystem))\\n325→\\n326→Letta’s filesystem allow you to easily connect your agents to external files, for example: research papers, reports, medical records, or any other data in common text formats (`.pdf`, `.txt`, `.md`, `.json`, etc).\\n327→Once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent will be able to use filesystem tools (`open_file`, `grep_file`, `search_file`) to browse the files to search for information.\\n328→\\n329→<details>\\n330→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n331→\\n332→### Python\\n333→```python\\n334→# create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n335→folder = client.folders.create(\\n336→    name=\\\"my_folder\\\"\\n337→)\\n338→\\n339→# upload a file into the folder\\n340→job = client.folders.files.upload(\\n341→    folder_id=folder.id,\\n342→    file=open(\\\"my_file.txt\\\", \\\"rb\\\")\\n343→)\\n344→\\n345→# wait until the job is completed\\n346→while True:\\n347→    job = client.jobs.retrieve(job.id)\\n348→    if job.status == \\\"completed\\\":\\n349→        break\\n350→    elif job.status == \\\"failed\\\":\\n351→        raise ValueError(f\\\"Job failed: {job.metadata}\\\")\\n352→    print(f\\\"Job status: {job.status}\\\")\\n353→    time.sleep(1)\\n354→\\n355→# once you attach a folder to an agent, the agent can see all files in it\\n356→client.agents.folders.attach(agent_id=agent.id, folder_id=folder.id)\\n357→\\n358→response = client.agents.messages.create(\\n359→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n360→    messages=[\\n361→        {\\n362→            \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n363→            \\\"content\\\": \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n364→        }\\n365→    ]\\n366→)\\n367→\\n368→for message in response.messages:\\n369→    print(message)\\n370→```\\n371→\\n372→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n373→```typescript\\n374→// create the folder (embeddings managed automatically by Letta Cloud)\\n375→const folder = await client.folders.create({\\n376→    name: \\\"my_folder\\\"\\n377→});\\n378→\\n379→// upload a file into the folder\\n380→const uploadJob = await client.folders.files.upload(\\n381→    createReadStream(\\\"my_file.txt\\\"),\\n382→    folder.id,\\n383→);\\n384→console.log(\\\"file uploaded\\\")\\n385→\\n386→// wait until the job is completed\\n387→while (true) {\\n388→    const job =             role: \\\"user\\\",\\n414→                content: \\\"What data is inside of my_file.txt?\\\"\\n415→            }\\n416→        ]\\n417→    }\\n418→);\\n419→\\n420→for (const message of response.messages) {\\n421→    console.log(message);\\n422→}\\n423→```\\n424→</details>\\n425→\\n426→## Long-running agents ([full guide](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/long-running))\\n427→\\n428→When agents need to execute multiple tool calls or perform complex operations (like deep research, data analysis, or multi-step workflows), processing time can vary significantly. Letta supports both a background mode (with resumable streaming) as well as an async mode (with polling) to enable robust long-running agent executions.\\n429→\\n430→\\n431→<details>\\n432→<summary>View code snippets</summary>\\n433→\\n434→### Python\\n435→```python\\n436→stream = client.agents.messages.create_stream(\\n437→    agent_id=agent_state.id,\\n438→    messages=[\\n439→      {\\n440→        \\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\",\\n441→        \\\"content\\\": \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n442→      }\\n443→    ],\\n444→    stream_tokens=True,\\n445→    background=True,\\n446→)\\n447→run_id = None\\n448→last_seq_id = None\\n449→for chunk in stream:\\n450→    if hasattr(chunk, \\\"run_id\\\") and hasattr(chunk, \\\"seq_id\\\"):\\n451→        run_id = chunk.run_id       # Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n452→        last_seq_id = chunk.seq_id  # Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n453→    print(chunk)\\n454→\\n455→# If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id:\\n456→for chunk in client.runs.stream(run_id, starting_after=last_seq_id):\\n457→    print(chunk)\\n458→```\\n459→\\n460→### TypeScript / Node.js\\n461→```typescript\\n462→const stream = await client.agents.messages.createStream({\\n463→    agentId: agentState.id,\\n464→    requestBody: {\\n465→        messages: [\\n466→            {\\n467→                role: \\\"user\\\",\\n468→                content: \\\"Run comprehensive analysis on this dataset\\\"\\n469→            }\\n470→        ],\\n471→        streamTokens: true,\\n472→        background: true,\\n473→    }\\n474→});\\n475→\\n476→let runId = null;\\n477→let lastSeqId = null;\\n478→for await (const chunk of stream) {\\n479→    if (chunk.run_id && chunk.seq_id) {\\n480→        runId = chunk.run_id;      // Save this to reconnect if your connection drops\\n481→        lastSeqId = chunk.seq_id; // Save this as your resumption point for cursor-based pagination\\n482→    }\\n483→    console.log(chunk);\\n484→}\\n485→\\n486→// If disconnected, resume from last received seq_id\\n487→for await (const chunk of client.runs.stream(runId, {startingAfter: lastSeqId})) {\\n488→    console.log(chunk);\\n489→}\\n490→```\\n491→</details>\\n492→\\n493→## Using local models\\n494→\\n495→Letta is model agnostic and supports using local model providers such as [Ollama](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/ollama) and [LM Studio](https://docs.letta.com/guides/server/providers/lmstudio). You can also easily swap models inside an agent after the agent has been created, by modifying the agent state with the new model provider via the SDK or in the ADE.\\n496→\\n497→## Development (only needed if you need to modify the server code)\\n498→\\n499→*Note: this repostory contains the source code for the core Letta service (API server), not the client SDKs. **Audit Result**

Checked `Z:/repos/deps/letta` at local HEAD `bb52a8900a79cf1378e6e9cdecf244b673a13a72` via `.git/refs/heads/main`. The two Fire 13 claims need revision.

**Claim 1: MCP Integration Status**

Status: **REFUTED**

Fire13 says Letta is not MCP-native and would require a custom MCP wrapper. The Letta repo has first-class MCP protocol support, not just incidental references:

- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:57` declares `mcp[cli]>=1.9.4`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:76` declares `fastmcp>=2.12.5`.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/base_client.py:4` imports MCP `ClientSession` and `Tool`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/base_client.py:83` lists tools via the MCP session; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/base_client.py:104` executes MCP tools.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/stdio_client.py:3` imports MCP stdio primitives; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/stdio_client.py:22` builds `StdioServerParameters`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/stdio_client.py:25` creates an MCP `ClientSession`.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/schemas/mcp_server.py:22`, `:31`, and `:47` define Stdio, SSE, and Streamable HTTP MCP server schemas; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/schemas/mcp_server.py:63` unions them.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/server/rest_api/routers/v1/mcp_servers.py:36` exposes `/v1/mcp-servers/`; `:47` says it adds an MCP server to Letta config; `:133` lists MCP server tools; `:163` runs an MCP tool; `:194` refreshes MCP server tools; `:215` exposes connect; `:236` says it connects to an MCP server with OAuth over SSE.

Nuance: I found production MCP **client/manager** support and REST API support for configuring and using external MCP servers. I did not find a production Letta-as-an-MCP-server implementation exposing Letta itself as MCP tools; the FastMCP server implementations I saw are test fixtures. But the Fire13 wording “MCP-native: NO” and “requires custom MCP wrapper” is too broad and is refuted by native MCP protocol support.

**Claim 2: PostgreSQL Backend Requirement**

Status: **REFUTED**

Fire13 says PostgreSQL is the backend requirement and adds a new infrastructure dependency. Letta supports PostgreSQL, but the repo does not show PostgreSQL as strictly required; it has SQLite defaults and explicit SQLite migration/code paths:

- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:258` says `pg_uri` defaults to none; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:259` sets `default_pg_uri = None`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:306` makes `pg_uri` optional.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:273` defines `DatabaseChoice`; `:274` is `POSTGRES`; `:275` is `SQLITE`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:493` returns PostgreSQL only if `letta_pg_uri_no_default` exists, otherwise SQLite.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/config.py:61`, `:66`, and `:71` default archival, recall, and metadata storage to `sqlite`.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/alembic/env.py:21` uses the PG URI when configured; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/alembic/env.py:26` otherwise sets `sqlalchemy.url` to a local SQLite DB.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/alembic/versions/2c059cad97cc_create_sqlite_baseline_schema.py:24` says the migration is only for SQLite; `:25` skips it when a PG URI exists; `:28` creates a SQLite schema aligned with the PostgreSQL state.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:89` defines a PostgreSQL optional dependency group; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:98` defines a SQLite optional dependency group.

**Audit Result**

Checked `Z:/repos/deps/letta` at local HEAD `bb52a8900a79cf1378e6e9cdecf244b673a13a72` via `.git/refs/heads/main`. The two Fire 13 claims need revision.

**Claim 1: MCP Integration Status**

Status: **REFUTED**

Fire13 says Letta is not MCP-native and would require a custom MCP wrapper. The Letta repo has first-class MCP protocol support, not just incidental references:

- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:57` declares `mcp[cli]>=1.9.4`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:76` declares `fastmcp>=2.12.5`.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/base_client.py:4` imports MCP `ClientSession` and `Tool`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/base_client.py:83` lists tools via the MCP session; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/base_client.py:104` executes MCP tools.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/stdio_client.py:3` imports MCP stdio primitives; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/stdio_client.py:22` builds `StdioServerParameters`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/services/mcp/stdio_client.py:25` creates an MCP `ClientSession`.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/schemas/mcp_server.py:22`, `:31`, and `:47` define Stdio, SSE, and Streamable HTTP MCP server schemas; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/schemas/mcp_server.py:63` unions them.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/server/rest_api/routers/v1/mcp_servers.py:36` exposes `/v1/mcp-servers/`; `:47` says it adds an MCP server to Letta config; `:133` lists MCP server tools; `:163` runs an MCP tool; `:194` refreshes MCP server tools; `:215` exposes connect; `:236` says it connects to an MCP server with OAuth over SSE.

Nuance: I found production MCP **client/manager** support and REST API support for configuring and using external MCP servers. I did not find a production Letta-as-an-MCP-server implementation exposing Letta itself as MCP tools; the FastMCP server implementations I saw are test fixtures. But the Fire13 wording “MCP-native: NO” and “requires custom MCP wrapper” is too broad and is refuted by native MCP protocol support.

**Claim 2: PostgreSQL Backend Requirement**

Status: **REFUTED**

Fire13 says PostgreSQL is the backend requirement and adds a new infrastructure dependency. Letta supports PostgreSQL, but the repo does not show PostgreSQL as strictly required; it has SQLite defaults and explicit SQLite migration/code paths:

- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:258` says `pg_uri` defaults to none; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:259` sets `default_pg_uri = None`; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/settings.py:306` makes `pg_uri` optional.

- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/letta/config.py:61`, `:66`, and `:71` default archival, recall, and metadata storage to `sqlite`.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/alembic/env.py:21` uses the PG URI when configured; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/alembic/env.py:26` otherwise sets `sqlalchemy.url` to a local SQLite DB.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/alembic/versions/2c059cad97cc_create_sqlite_baseline_schema.py:24` says the migration is only for SQLite; `:25` skips it when a PG URI exists; `:28` creates a SQLite schema aligned with the PostgreSQL state.
- `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:89` defines a PostgreSQL optional dependency group; `Z:/repos/deps/letta/pyproject.toml:98` defines a SQLite optional dependency group.

But the claim “PostgreSQL required” is not supported because the repo has explicit SQLite defaults and SQLite migration support.
